# Dora Ella Newnam Stone

*A documented life, 1871–1926. Compiled from every research chat in this project, 27–30 October 2026. Living descendants are not named. Facts below the line of proof are labelled in the text. Nothing here is family lore presented as fact.*

### Preface

This book is a biography, not a pedigree abstract. It answers one question: what can be documented, under the Genealogical Proof Standard, of the life of Dora Ella Newnam, later Dora Stone, who died at Ola, Yell County, Arkansas, at noon on Monday, 23 August 1926.

Sixteen Cursor chats produced the evidence. The original mobile session opened the case and read the Arkansas death certificate the family still holds. Later sessions recovered the 1880 childhood schedule that free transcripts had missed, walked statewide Soundex reels for 1910 and 1920, tested a Peebles third-marriage hypothesis and set it aside, and photographed sixty-one frames of National Archives microfilm. This volume folds those chats into one narrative. The case dossier remains the working file; this book is the public reading of it.

Two marriages are documented. A later Peebles husband is not. The year 1868 on the death certificate is wrong. Where she spent April 1910 and January 1920 is still unknown. Those limits are part of the story.

Claim tags such as C003 and source tags such as S010 point back to the evidence catalog. They are not decoration.

---

## Chapter 1. The girl on Sturgeon Creek

Lee County, Kentucky, was ten years old when the census taker James J. Hampton reached dwelling 46 in District 59. The county had been cut from Breathitt, Estill, Owsley and Wolfe around the Three Forks of the Kentucky River. On 5 June 1880 Hampton listed a farmer, Elias Newnam, thirty-eight, and his wife Mary, thirty-eight, both Kentucky-born, with six children still at home: William, twenty-one, at the plough; Malissa, sixteen; Francis, fourteen; Mary, ten; Dora, eight; and Catharine, six. [C003] [S010]

That single line — Dora, daughter, eight, Kentucky — is the childhood record. A Soundex clerk later copied it onto a continuation card, code N-550, volume 18, enumeration district 59, sheet 6, line 11: “Newnam, Dora D 8 Kentucky.” [S001] [S002] A child aged eight on 1 June 1880 was born between June 1871 and June 1872. The 1900 census would give the month as August 1871. The 1885 marriage return would call her fourteen. Those three independent records agree. [C001] [S005] [S004]

Her death certificate, written by a son fifty-five years later, says 11 August 1868. That child would have been eleven in June 1880. The year 1868 is disproved. The day, 11 August, appears only on the certificate and is possible, not proved. This book uses August 1871. [C002] [S003]

The parents on the 1880 schedule are the couple the certificate would name: Elias Newnam and Mary Smallwood. [C004] Neighbors on the same page were Newnams as well — Joseph, thirty-eight, next door, and John, sixty, in the household after. Elias, known as Doll, had been bugler of Company C, 14th Kentucky Cavalry, in 1862–63. Mary Ann Smallwood, born April 1842, came from the Owsley Smallwoods. Those military and cemetery facts were gathered in the first research chat and were not re-imaged later. They remain probable, not re-proved from originals in this file.

Ten years earlier, on the 1870 Travellers Rest schedule (USGenNet transcript **7 June**; original enumerator sheet **17 June**), Elias and Mary were in Owsley County with William, Malissa, Frances and a one-year-old Mary. There was no Dora. That absence fits an 1871 birth. [C067] [S048] [S231]

Kentucky did not yet keep statewide birth registers the way it would after 1911. The surviving Lee County vital-statistics birth extracts for **1874–1878** (USGenNet Kendrick transcriptions) contain **no** line for Dora or Dora Ella Newnam. They do contain her younger sister: **5 August 1874**, **Katty Newnam**, female, born Lee County, daughter of **Elias Newman** born Clark County and **Mary Smallwood** born Owsley County. The clerk spelled the surname Newman; the family used Newnam on the census. That register names the same parents the 1880 schedule and 1926 death certificate would — and it fixes Catharine’s birth year independently of the census age. [C044] [C251] [S230] Dora’s August **1871** month and year therefore rest on the **1880 age 8**, the **1900 August 1871**, and the **1885 age 14**, not on a civil birth certificate. [C001]

A compiled Boone descent through Leah Scholl sits on WikiTree and on cemetery markers. This case has not independently re-proved those steps. They stay speculative.

## Chapter 2. Thursday at Doll Newnam’s

On Thursday, 10 December 1885, at Doll Newnam’s house, J. E. Dunagin married Samuel P. Newnam, sixteen, farmer, to Dora Ella Newnam, fourteen. Witnesses: Doll Newnam and Link Mays. It was a first marriage for both. Samuel had been born in Owsley County; his father, the clerk wrote, in Clark, his mother in Owsley. Dora had been born in Lee. The clerk put both of her parents’ births in Owsley — a slip. Elias was born in Clark County on 18 April 1842. Teenage brides and county clerks often confuse “where we farmed” with “where he was born.” [C010] [S004]

Published Lee marriage books 1–15 name **Link Mays only once**: as witness beside Doll Newnam at this wedding. He is not a groom or bride in those transcriptions. [C127] [S116] Two years later, at Marion Durbin’s on 2 January 1889, **Jiles Mays** witnessed with **Dilalrd Newnam**. That Jiles is probably the farm labourer, twenty, in Andrew Mayse’s Proctor house in 1870, the twenty-four-year-old who applied to marry Elizabeth Bowman on 22 March 1873, and the **Giles Mays** (1849, son of Andrew and Rachel, husband of Lizzie Bowman) copied at William Mays Cemetery on Elk Creek — a different man from Link. A bracketed death year of 1904 on that cemetery list is a transcriber’s note, not a proved date. [C125] [C132] [C145] [S121] [S125] [S129]

The 1900 Sturgeon transcript lists eleven **Mays** households and **no** given name **Link**. [S116] Absence in 1900 now has a paper trail, not only a nickname. In Booneville in 1880, **Louisa Pendergrass**, eighteen, was still single in Cordy Pendergrass’s house; two dwellings away, **Larkin T. Mayse**, twenty, was a single labourer in Stephen McPhearson’s. [C136] [C137] [S128] They married after that census. On 23 March 1911, at Elizabeth Brandenburg’s in Idamay, Geo. Pliman married **Ida Mays**, seventeen. The clerk wrote her parents: **Larkin Mays, Lee County**, and **Louisa Pendergrass, Owsley County**. One witness was **Sshelia Newnam**. [C142] [S132]

The 1927 Kentucky Court of Appeals said the rest in a sentence. *Brandenburg v. Petroleum Exploration*, 218 Ky. 557: **“The appellant, Louisa Brandenburg, is the widow of Larkin Mays, who died intestate on June 8, 1895,”** leaving four infant children and a Lee County tract worth less than a thousand dollars, which she still occupied as a homestead. [C139] [C141] [S131] The Cordy Pendergrass Cemetery stone says **8 July 1895**. The court says **8 June**. This book prints both and does not invent a third date. [C140]

On 1 June 1900 she was already the widow **Eliza MAYS** at Sturgeon household 26: born December 1861, four children living. Whitly, June 1884; Martha, November 1886; and Ida, October 1894, were in the house. A hired hand, John Brandenburgh, March 1882, boarded there. The fourth child, **Vesta**, had married Hardin Gray at Liza Mays’s on 4 October 1897 and was two dozen households away: Hardin, Vestie, and baby Rosco at household 85. Louisa was not in that house. [C161] [C162] [C166] [C191] [S233] Vestie Bell Gray died 21 October 1958. The Lee County death-certificate extract names her parents **Larkin Mays** and **Eliza Pendergrass**. The Gray Cemetery stone on Highway 587 says 9 September 1882; the 1900 census said September 1881. [C192] [C193] [S164] Martha’s 1927 death certificate names Larkin Mays and Louisa Pendergrass and gives her birth as 1 November 1886. [C164] On 29 August 1903 S. P. Farmer married Martha at **her mother’s, Eliza Brandenburg’s**. [C165] Burtie Brandenburg was born 15 July 1903, so the second marriage was in place by midsummer. [C143] [S133]

The 1910 census has two traps with the same names. In Sturgeon family 97 a **John Brandenburg**, twenty-nine, lives with a wife the Miracode clerk wrote as Louisa (the schedule itself reads **Susan**), twenty-six, and daughters Ida, six, and Bertie, five. Those ages are twenty years too young for Louisa, Ida, and Burtie. [C177] [C178] The real Martha is in the same precinct, family 251, sheet 13 B: **Samuel Farmer**, thirty-three, married six years; **Martha**, twenty-three; Flora, five; Everett, three. There is no mother-in-law in that house. [C182] Two families away on the Miracode—and on the **original** census at family **248**, sheet **13 B**—**Hardin Gray**, thirty-four, lives with **Vesta**, twenty-seven, and four children. Louisa is not in that house. Sheet **14 B** is blank; that is the only gap on the bound ED **99** roll. [C184] [C166] [C225] [S236] Kentucky’s 1910 Mays soundex (M-200) has no Lee Louisa, Eliza, Ida, or Whitley as a head. A Clark County Whitley Mats, twenty-four, boarding with Zack Nopper, is a different man. [C185] [C186] [C187] [C188] Patsey Farley’s 1937 obituary still called her sister **Mrs. Louisa Mays, Cressmont**. [C130] [S101] Cressmont is a post office, not a 1910 precinct. [C173] In April 1910 Louisa is not in Proctor under either married name. [C154] The 1911 witness **Sshelia Newnam** is not Celia McGuire, fifty-five, née Newman, in Christopher McGuire’s Proctor house that year. [C156] SortedByName’s Louisa Brandenburg block has no 1951 Lee or Owsley death; the family’s 13 April 1951 date stays family information. [C157]

Larkin was twenty-four and living on the December 1885 wedding day and dead before June 1900. **Link** is a common nickname for Larkin. That is enough for a possible identity, not a merge: no paper yet uses both names. [C131] Published Owsley marriage vital-statistics years — 1853–59, 1874–78, 1903–10 — do not contain their license. The gap is 1879–1902. [C148] [S126]

Mays were already in District 59 in 1880. The mortality schedule Hampton filed for that district lists **Mary Mays**, eighty-three, of household **102**, dead of paralysis in January. [C133] [S120] William Mays Cemetery, just over the Lee line at Cross Roads, has **Mary A. “Pollie” Mays**, 1800–1880, née Gross. Ages 80 and 83 are close; the birthplaces do not yet match. She is a possible neighbour, not a merge. [C147] Sheets 1–2 around John and Elias do not show household 102; the original page is still blocked on Internet Archive. [L136]

A separate **Newnam Cemetery** at Pebworth — also called Newman or Newman-Combs, a Kentucky Landmark, 101 Find a Grave memorials — is the earlier clan yard, not High View on Spencer Ridge. No Samuel P. or Dora listing turned up in the free sources opened this pass. [C150] [S130]

The original marriage page (FamilySearch Q2QD-PZHL) has not been seen from this workspace. The reading is from Debi Kendrick’s Lee County Book 2 transcription. The fact of the marriage is proved; palaeography of the original page remains an open retrieval.

Samuel was family. On the first page of the same 1880 district, dwelling 3, taken 1 June, a boy named Samuel, eleven, son, lived with John Newnam, sixty-nine, farmer, born in Kentucky, father born in North Carolina, and Mary, fifty-seven, with Eli, sixteen, Susan, twenty, and Hannah, nine. [C060] [C061] [S040] Age eleven in June 1880 is exactly the June 1869 birth of the 1900 husband and the sixteen-year-old of December 1885. Identification of that boy as Dora’s husband is probable.

Ten years before that, on 29 June 1870, Sturgeon Precinct household 19 held Newman, John, fifty, farm labourer; Elizabeth, twenty-five, keeping house; and Samuel, one, with eight others. [C062] [S042] That Elizabeth is the right age for the widow who later took Pearl. The 1870 and 1880 households are not the same house with a bad enumerator. John ages fifty to sixty-nine in ten years. The wives are Elizabeth, twenty-five, and Mary, fifty-seven. The children’s names do not carry over. Conflict #9 stays open. This book does not print “son of Elizabeth” or “son of Mary” as fact.

A second Samuel, about thirteen, on sheet 2 of the same district is Samuel McQueary, not a Newnam. [C063] There is only one Samuel Newnam of the right age in ED 59.

WikiTree gives Samuel the middle name Parriott. No record in this case uses it. The initial P. is proved; the expansion is lore.

## Chapter 3. Seven born, five living

They stayed on Sturgeon Creek. On 1 June 1900 Samuel, born June 1869, married fourteen years, and Dora E., born August 1871, twenty-eight, stood at dwelling 67 with five children: McCoy, April 1890; Mary M., June 1892; Pearl, March 1895; Rissa, May 1897; and John H., August 1899, nine months old. Dora reported seven children born, five living. [C011] [C020–C025] [S005] The two who died are unnamed in any record yet read. Two deaths in seven births is a household fact, not a county rate.

The original 1900 population schedule is not among the sixty-one plates. The household is known from a reliable county transcription (S005). Recovering the microfilm page remains outstanding.

Pearl later opened a Social Security account in her married name, Pearl P. Jackson, also known as Pearl P. Newnam. She named her parents Sam Newnam and Dora Newnam, and her birthplace Banford, Lee County, 15 March 1895. Banford was the post office on Sturgeon Creek from 1902 to 1934. She died in Shelby County, Tennessee, on 19 September 1980. [S007] Mary Magdalene’s account names Sam P. and Dora Newnam and Beattyville, 12 June 1893 — a year off the 1900 census, a common childhood discrepancy. She is later Mary M. Bunch. [S006]

Those NUMIDENT files were read in the first research chat. They close identity for two daughters. They do not locate the mother after 1900.

---

## Places: 1900 to death

This section states only what records pin Dora’s **physical location**. Confidence labels match the dossier (`06-dossier.md`).

**1 June 1900 — PROVEN.** Sturgeon precinct, Lee County, Kentucky, dwelling 67/67, with husband Samuel P. Newnam and five living children. [C011] [S005]

**1900–April 1910 — PROBABLE Kentucky, place unknown.** Samuel is not a Kentucky Newman head in April 1910; Pearl is in Owsley with widowed grandmother Elizabeth — consistent with Samuel’s death before that census, but **no death certificate, obituary, or burial record** names him or a place. Lee vital-statistics gaps for 1905–1910 and published cemetery transcripts are **nil** for Samuel P. [C012] [C090] [C110] An August 1904 Lee license for **S. P. Newnam** × Rosa L. Banks is **not merged** with Samuel; if it were the same man, Lee County would still be the last documented venue for those initials — not Dora’s whereabouts. [C203]

**April 1910 — UNKNOWN (honest gap).** After statewide Soundex and original-schedule negatives in Kentucky, Arkansas, Missouri, Oklahoma, and Texas, plus Tennessee Coy/McCoy negatives and Peebles negatives, she is **not** located under Newman, Newnam, Stone, or Peebles at thirty-nine. She is **not** on the original Sturgeon ED 99 schedule as a Newnam; the 1900 dwelling 67 site is reoccupied. [C030] [C088] [C033] USGenNet transcripts for Lee’s Proctor, Fincastle, and New Virginia precincts plus all **seven** published Owsley districts are **nil** for P001. [C204] **Beattyville ED 91–98** originals on NARA **T624-478** (full PDF bracket **490–548**) show **no** P001 line (**C216**); a **Stone, Dora**, 32, wife of **Jas P. Stone** in ED 98 is a **different woman**. [C217] [S227] **Yell County, Arkansas** population on NARA **T624-68** (IA **`13thcensus1910po0068unit`**) — including **Ola Road** / **Rose Creek** sector sheets read — shows **no** **Newnam/Newman/Stone** + **Dora/Ella** ~35–42 b. Kentucky merge (**C219**), consistent with prior **AR N-550** nil (**C032**). [S228] The **1910** Kentucky Soundex **Ella** wife-on-husband reel remains unread here. [B044] / **L180**

**January 1920 — UNKNOWN for P001; FAN in two states.** She is **not** in Arkansas or Kentucky Soundex as Dora Stone or Dora Newman ~48. Coy is at **Danville**, Yell County, Arkansas, with wife Letha; Dora is **not** in that house. [C032] [C207] Her father Elias T. Newman, widowed, is in **Beattyville**, Lee County, in C. B. Burke’s postmaster household — Dora is **not** there. [C091] [C209] Tom A. Stone, widowed, is in **Howell Township, Perry County, Arkansas** — adjacent timber country to Carter Cove, but that is **his** address, not hers. [C050] [C145] **ED 200** (Ola / Ward Twp) was read in full (**S225**); **P001 is not on that enumeration** (**C213**). **ED 191** (Lamar / Plainview / Carter Cove sector) was **partially** read on the same reel; **no** Mrs. **T. A. Stone** ~48 b. Kentucky with son **McCoy** on pages examined (**C215**). [S226] Near-miss **Dora Stone** lines in ED 200 and ED 191 were **rejected** as other households. [C214] [C215] The **1920** Kentucky N-550 **Ella** tab lists **Newman, Ella**, 48, b. Kentucky, **Shelby County** only — **rejected** as P001 (**C218**). [S229]

**18 February 1917 — PROVEN event, PROBABLE inference for mother.** McCoy Newnam married Letha Viola Sullivan at **Ola**, Yell County. [C027] [S024] That is the first **documented Arkansas event** for this family. It does not state that Dora stood at the courthouse; it makes **western Arkansas** plausible from 1917 onward.

**1920–August 1926 — PROBABLE Lamar / Carter Cove sector, Yell County.** No census line names her as Mrs. Stone. ## Chapter 4. The years the census lost

Samuel disappears from free records. WikiTree’s 24 February 1906 is unsourced lore and is not printed here as a death date. [C012] [S030] What is not lore is the next sighting of a child.

In April 1910 Pearl, fifteen, was in Owsley County, Buck Creek, household 141, with her widowed paternal grandmother Elizabeth Newman, sixty-one, who reported seven children born and two living, and with Susan Angle, forty-four, a widow. Dora is not in that house. [C070] [S041] [S051] Elizabeth’s 1922 will, proved in 1934, left everything to granddaughter Pearl Jackson and her husband Clyde and named Clyde executor. [S054] Pearl married Clyde Jackson; their daughters Margaret E. (29 June 1917) and Fannie B. (11 March 1919) were registered in Lee County with mother Pearl Newman. By 1920 Clyde, twenty-six, and Pearl, twenty-four, were back on Sturgeon Creek with Estella and Fannie.

The grandmother who took Pearl was a widow. Combined with the empty Kentucky Soundex slot for Samuel P. Newnam as a head, that makes Samuel’s death before April 1910 probable. The place and the day are unknown. He is not in the Lee County death index for 1911–1929, not in the 1912–1929 certificate extracts, not in the surviving Lee vital-statistics dumps for 1874–1878 or 1900–1904, not on the Proctor Cemetery transcript, not on the 2001 High View / Newman Cemetery transcript, and not in the Booneville Association obituaries of 1883–1904. The published Lee will transcript index lists no Samuel P. Newnam will; Elizabeth’s will names Pearl only. [C232] [C233] Lee County has no inventoried death register for 1905–1910 (KDLA lists 1874–1878, 1900–1904, then 1911–1967). [C110] KDLA’s county inventory nonetheless holds Lee probate-related volumes for 1873–2004 — administrations and settlements, not just wills — and those reels have not been searched for Samuel P. in this case. [C243] [S250] Owsley’s 1907–1910 vital-statistics dumps have been read: Samuel P. is not in them. The only Newnam is Lester, thirteen months, who died at Pebworth on 15 April 1909; parents Robert and Sallie Newnam — not merged with Samuel. [C108] [C109] The remaining Kentucky search is unmarked graves, on-site church minutes, and unread probate film.

Dora herself is in no Kentucky N-550 card under Dora. McCoy is in no Kentucky N-550 card under McCoy. [C030] [C031] Their parents Elias — the enumerator wrote “Elisa” — and Mary (Smallwood) Newnam appear at Proctor precinct, ED 100, dwelling 19/20. Dora is not in their line. [C075]

She was not still on Sturgeon Creek under Newnam. The original 1910 Sturgeon schedule, ED 99, still has Newnams: Richard and Sarah, Reuben and Nannie, John and Ollie, Harry and Albert as Ross in-laws, Elijah Newnan, and a different John H. Newman with a grown son Malcum. None of them is Dora, Samuel, McCoy, or the 1899 child. The 1900 dwelling 67 site is occupied by Hamilton on one sheet and Garrett on another. [C088] [C106]

Statewide negatives close the obvious surnames and states. Arkansas has no Coy between Cora and Dan, no Dora before the E-names, no McCoy between Mayme and Melvina. Missouri Soundex has no Coy, no McCoy, and no Dora ~39 as a Newman head — but **Bollinger County** population schedules place **Letha Sullivan** (age **12**, servant) and **Newnam** in-laws in **John Sullivan**’s house in Liberty Township (**C244**–**C245**); the **S-415 Sullivan** Soundex reel (**S253**) is **nil** for Bollinger and for **LETHA** on cards (**C247**). Liberty ED **4** sheets 1–4 are **nil** for Sullivan, Letha, and Newnam (**C260**). Liberty ED **5** sheets 1–14 are the same nil (**C265**). A **Dora E.** on ED 5 sheet 10 is **Abraham Copley**’s wife, born October 1873 in Missouri — not her (**C266**). Two same-name women in the same county are **not** her: **Dora E. Stone**, thirty-seven, wife of **Henry C. Stone** in Crooked Creek (**C249**), and **Dora Newnam**, thirty-six, born **Indiana**, wife of **John H. Newnam** in Fillmore (**C250**). **P001** is still absent from that ED sector as a Kentucky-born woman of thirty-nine (**C246**). She was not Mrs. Peebles. Kentucky’s 1910 P-142 block has no Dora of thirty-nine as a head and no wife named Dora on any household card. The only D-window near-hit is Edora Peoples, sixty-five, a Tennessee-born sister-in-law in Graves County. Dora Stone died at Ola of apoplexy after one day, attended by Dr. Thomas W. Beal. The statewide Arkansas death index and its Ancestry mirror are notorious for misreading given names. You reported that this death was indexed as **Posa Stone** — a common misfile for **Dora** — while the certificate face still reads Dora Stone, certificate **1524**. Treat **Newman** and **Newnam** as the same family on every index pass; Carter Cove and Coy’s informant line already spell the maiden name both ways. Coy Newnam of Ola was informant. He named her spouse T. A. Stone and her parents E. T. Newnam and Mary Smallwood, and he sent her the next day to Carters Chapel. Daniels and Cossey, copying Carter Cove Cemetery on 5 February 1993, found “Newman, Dora” without dates — the name of the husband of her youth, and of the son who buried her. Nearby they copied “Stone, Thomas | 1845 | 1925.” [C016] [S078]

At Carter Cemetery (Carter Cove), Find a Grave’s **cemetery search** (not the individual memorial pages, which Cloudflare blocked from this workspace) lists **Dora Newman** (memorial 97907837) with **no dates** on the listing — matching the 1993 spelling under her maiden name — and **Thomas Stone** (97907931) as **1845–1925**, beside her in the same yard. Those listings are authored derivatives (**S117–S118**, claim **C129**), not vital records, but they align with the certificate and the copyists’ lines. The compiled yellcountyobits cemetery index (extract dated 29 January 2013), captured 30 October 2026, repeats the same two rows: **Newman, Dora | no dates | Carter Cove Cemetery, Plainview** and **Stone, Thomas | 1845 | 1925 | Carter Cove Cemetery, Plainview**. [C282] [C283] [S268] [S269] No photograph of either stone is in the case file.

The *Yell County Journal* at Ola for late August and early September 1926 has not been read. The only free *Beattyville Enterprise* issues on Internet Archive item `beatty` are six numbers from 1924, 1925, 1928, and 1932 — **1926 is not among them**. [C293] University of Kentucky’s film catalog lists 1909–1911, 1915, 1924–1925, 1928, and 1932 — again **no 1926**. [S271] Internet Archive Solr returned **zero** items for *Yell County Journal* 1926 and **zero** for a 1926 *Enterprise*. ### Pass 70. John and Cora in Woodford, 1950

They turn up in Woodford in 1950: John, seventy, and Cora, fifty-six. The 1926 Callie Newnam on the Lee death extract is a different woman. She died 27 April 1926 at Proctor — a second extract says Idamay — aged thirty years and one month, parents Sam Moore and Mary Brandenburg. Dorothy Newnam, one year old, died 6 October 1926 at Idamay; her extract names father Everett Newnam and mother Callie Moore. Those two independent identifiers make Callie Moore Newnam **proven** as Everett’s wife. She is **not** Callie (Newnam) Spicer of Lower Buffalo, who was still living in May 1944. Conflict #21. [C355] [C356] [C357] [S331]

Melissa, the sixteen-year-old in Elias’s 1880 house, died 11 April 1957 as Melissa Newnam Estes Miller, ninety-three, daughter of Elias T. and Mary Ann Smallwood Newman, born 22 November 1863. The *Enterprise* of 18 April 1957 and the 1957 year-file agree: funeral 14 April at Beattyville Christian Church; buried Riverview. Survivors: son Herman Estes of Brasstown, North Carolina; stepson Johnnie E. Miller; granddaughter Ruby Booth. Same-person versus 1880 Malissa: parents plus birth year. Dora is not named — she had been dead thirty-one years. The 1939 living-sister list (Frankie Jones, Mary Peters, Melissa Eastes) is consistent; the 1957 notice does not name Frankie or Mary as living. [C358] [S332] [C003]

He is **not** Everett’s brother Frank the contractor of Berea, who was still living on 13 December 1939. Conflict #22. Dunigan and Sirena Porter, to Ladona Newman, sixteen, of Delvinta, daughter of **Shelby Newman** and **Grace Brandenburg**. The *Danville Democrat* of 1899–1927 (LCCN SN89051010) was still publishing that August, but the recorded holdings are 2 April 1908 and 15 July 1915 — no 1926 issue. The *Dardanelle Post-Dispatch* (LCCN SN89051008, 190?–1976) is the surviving weekly: its film at the Arkansas State Archives, the Dardanelle library, Pope County Library, and the University of Arkansas **includes** August 1926. Those reels have not been read. Internet Archive holds none of those 1926 issues. A Chronicling America search for “Stone Ola Arkansas” in 1926 returns the *Osceola Times* and other counties — not Yell. Lee obituaries S, D, and L have no Dora Stone notice for 1926. ## The children, in brief

Two children born to Dora and Samuel died before 1 June 1900. They have no names in any record yet read.

**McCoy “Coy” Newnam**, born April 1890, married Letha Viola Sullivan at Ola on 18 February 1917. In 1920 they were at Danville, Yell County. In 1930 they were back in Buck Creek, Owsley County — **Newman, McCoy**, forty, with Edith, twenty-nine, of Missouri. That head is **probable** Dora’s son. [C561] [C562] [S409] He is the 1926 informant. Kentucky’s death index for 1931–1989 has no McCoy or Coy Newman or Newnam. [C565] He is not an Owsley head in 1940 or 1950. [C574] [C575] A California NUMIDENT row for **MCCOY, NEWMAN**, born 13 April 1890, is **possible** only and is not merged. [C576] Living descendants are not named here.

**Mary Magdalene Newnam**, June 1892 on the census, 12 June 1893 Beattyville on her Social Security account, later Mary M. Bunch. Marriage and death are not closed in free sources: Lee marriage books have no Bunch row; NUMIDENT lists her **deceased** but gives **no death date**; the public Social Security Death Index API returned no match for SSN **410-19-2658**; Tennessee archives and Knox marriage indexes were not reachable from the research VM (**R007**, **L257**). [C158]

**Pearl P. Newnam**, 15 March 1895, Banford, later Pearl P. Jackson, died 19 September 1980, Shelby County, Tennessee. The 1910 grandmother household, the 1922 Elizabeth Newnam will, and the NUMIDENT file make her identification the strongest of the five.

**Rissa / Rissie**, May 1897. After the 1900 census she does not reappear on the Kentucky child Soundex pass or in Pearl’s 1910 Owsley household. On 15 April 1910 a **Newman, Rissie**, servant, fifteen, sits in **Edney, Sam**’s Heidelberg house — **proven** as a line, **possible** Dora’s daughter, **not merged** (age slack). [C738] [C760] A Rissie Dishman Beaty of Overton County, Tennessee (1887–1926) is **rejected**. [C927] An unsourced WikiTree profile (**Newnam-46**) names her **Rissie (Newnam) Pelfrey**, born at **Pebworth**, died **13 May 1980** in **Knoxville, Tennessee**, with **Samuel Parriott Newnam** as father — a **possible** lead only, not printed here as proved death. [C230] Free Tennessee death and obituary indexes searched from this case did not independently confirm that row. [C231] Lee Marriage Books 5–15 have no Rissie or Rissa Newnam bride; a 1920 Rissa Newton, seventeen, is a different woman. [C134] A Rissie Pelfrey Maxey born 1882 is a different woman.

**John H. Newnam**, August 1899. Herbert Newman of Lee County, Virginia, was rejected. The Kentucky statewide death index (Reclaim The Records, 1911–1990) has no John H. Newnam death in Lee consistent with an August 1899 birth. [C234] [C237] [C238] Lee Marriage Book 12 has a different **John Newnam**, age twenty-five in 1917, son of John and Serilda — rejected. [C239] Genealogy Trails’ Lee County WWI draft transcript (1,126 cards) lists no Newnam, Newman, or McCoy registrant; other men born in 1899 appear on the September 1918 Beattyville board, but not John under that surname. [C240] [C241] [S248] A cross-border free transcript pass (Owsley, Estill, Arkansas Perry/Yell, Knox KYGenWeb, Missouri Digital Heritage WWI biographies) also returned no Newnam/Newman draft row. [C242] [S249] The official National Archives M1509 pamphlet now maps Lee to **KY45**, Owsley to **KY74**, and Estill to **KY24**. The cards themselves have not been read. [C1021]

## How to read the plates

Part II of the PDF prints every JPEG preserved in this case, sixty-one frames, in chronological order of the record. Four roles appear in the captions:

- **Family hit** — names Dora or a documented relative.
- **Rejected same-name** — a Dora, Samuel, Thomas, or similar who fails the same-person test.
- **Negative search** — the alphabetical or dwelling slot where she would appear. She is not there. Negatives are evidence.
- **Context** — an adjacent page or divider, kept so a later reader can verify the search window.

The plates are National Archives microfilm via the Internet Archive, inverted and contrast-enhanced where the scan required it. They are not studio photographs. A family portrait of a woman identified as Dora was supplied in the original chat and is not in this repository; treat that likeness as family identification, speculative until independently confirmed.

Charts of pedigree, descendants, and lifespan follow the plates. Dashed lines mean probable. Dotted lines, had any been drawn, would mean speculative. No living person born after 1926 appears.
